# BT20 audit

## Status

All 102 BT20 cards carry current, independently reproduced 10/10 evidence: 1020/1020 points on the 2026-09-10 re-audit ledger, closed by commit 6e76b258c. This resolves the contradiction that BT20 carried until now. `docs/audits/BT20-AUDIT.md` and `apps/api/src/cards/BT20/AUDIT.md` (both commit 4a84c8d65, 2026-09-06) carried a banner saying the collection was incomplete and under independent revalidation, while their bodies claimed 102/102 verified. That revalidation is the `internal-docs/audits/BT20/revalidation/` set, which completed on 2026-09-06 (`DELIVERY.md`, commit 021ef1445) and was integrated into main through commit 822e75a9f. A further independent re-audit then ran on 2026-09-10 from base 5b3d42c968, did not inherit any earlier score, found and fixed two red cards at baseline (BT20-003 and BT20-028), and closed at 102/102. The banner is stale: the set is complete. The 2026-09-10 re-audit is the winning source; both 2026-09-06 documents are superseded.

## Gates

Commands and results below are copied from the winning report, the 2026-09-10 re-audit.

### Run evidence

Source: `docs/audits/BT20-reaudit/RUN.md` (last commit 6e76b258c, 2026-09-10).

#### 2026-09-10 start

- Fresh Orca worktree `audit-bt20-luna-20260910` created from `origin/main` at `5b3d42c9680a40dd094444d18fdd5d58c8184358`.
- Historical BT20 scores are not inherited. Current evidence must be independently reproduced.
- User requested Luna workers; this overrides the audit skill's default worker model.
- Machine guardrails: 16 GiB RAM, low disk headroom, at most three Luna workers, focused tests single-worker, broad tests and typecheck serialized by the coordinator.
- `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile`: exit 0; 423 packages reused and no downloads.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/shared build`: exit 0.
- `pnpm effects:check:set -- --set BT20 --base 5b3d42c9680a40dd094444d18fdd5d58c8184358`: 102 records synchronized, zero BT20 changes, zero semantic or byte changes outside BT20.
- Baseline BT20 collection: 103 files; 958 passed and 4 failed. Failures: BT20-003 (one stale `orderTriggers` milestone) and BT20-028 (three assertions target permanents already removed from the board).
- Workspace typecheck did not run because the chained baseline stopped at the red collection gate.

#### First accepted lanes

- BT20-003: corrected an impossible no-Tamer fixture; coordinator reproduced 6/6 focused tests.
- BT20-028: corrected source-stack/De-Digivolve fixtures and assertions; coordinator reproduced 12/12 focused tests.
- BT20-005: replaced an illegal Digi-Egg deck fixture; coordinator reproduced 5/5 focused tests.
- BT20-084: corrected exact Sistermon Ciel evolution matching, added controller boundaries, and replaced an illegal Digi-Egg fixture; coordinator reproduced 12/12 focused tests.
- BT20-095: restricted the Security play target to Chronicle Digimon and added a non-Digimon negative; coordinator reproduced 15/15 focused tests.
- Catalog BT20-098 was corrected locally from the pre-errata “up to 9” text to the authoritative exact-9 errata supported by Q4439.
- Static triage confirmed 102/102 exclusive `registerIrCard` registrations and zero TypeScript suppression directives, while identifying remaining illegal fixtures, exact-name risks, and injected-timing proof gaps for subsequent card lanes.

#### Re-audit checkpoint — 27 cards

- Ledger: 216/1020 provisional; 27/102 cards independently scored 8/10 with delivery gates pending.
- Every accepted card has a per-card report and a coordinator-reproduced focused suite.
- Exact-name IR corrections accepted for BT20-007, BT20-010, BT20-012, BT20-015, BT20-076, and BT20-084; BT20-008/013/014 name-containing wording was independently confirmed to require substring matching.
- BT20-011 now explicitly sources its DNA destination from hand; Q6017 proof confirms the `then` clause continues after ExVeemon leaves without incorrectly making the removed ExVeemon a DNA material.
- BT20-091 no longer uses injected timing or manual DP mutation; its watcher/replacement proof follows public turn/evolution/play flows.
- Illegal deck/security Digi-Egg fixtures were removed from accepted lanes including BT20-002, 005, 011, 013, 045, 057, 067, 069, 075, 076, 081, 084, 090, and 091.
- Worker-proposed tests that encoded false engine assumptions were rejected and corrected: alternate-route requests may legally fall back to a normal route (BT20-010/069), and a removed ExVeemon is not later materialized by Q6017 (BT20-011).

#### Re-audit checkpoint — 47 cards

- Ledger: 376/1020 provisional; 47/102 cards independently scored 8/10 with delivery gates pending.
- BT20-016 through BT20-036 are fully re-reviewed except BT20-028, which was accepted in the first checkpoint; all focused suites are coordinator-reproduced green.
- Natural-flow rewrites uncovered and corrected stale assumptions about normal security checks (BT20-021), token removal rather than trash placement (BT20-017), attack legality/OPT reset (BT20-018/027), and printed evolution costs (BT20-036).
- Additional exact-match corrections landed for BT20-016, 022, 023, 024, 025, 027, 029, and 032.
- BT20-027 required a second rejection cycle because its first reported repair did not change the failing behavior; acceptance occurred only after the actual 18/18 focused command passed.

#### Collection closeout — 102 cards

- Ledger reached 816/1020 before delivery gates: 102/102 cards independently scored 8/10 with a per-card report and coordinator-reproduced focused suite.
- BT20 collection gate: 103/103 files and 1035/1035 tests passed with one worker and no file parallelism.
- Engine mechanism gate: 137/137 files and 2070/2070 tests passed with one worker and no file parallelism.
- Workspace typecheck passed after strict typing corrections in audit tests.
- Static guards passed: 102 exclusive `registerIrCard` modules, zero `registerCard`, zero TypeScript suppressions, and clean `git diff --check`.
- Effects reconciliation synchronized 102 records and reported 37 semantic BT20 changes with zero semantic or byte changes outside BT20 against base `5b3d42c9680a40dd094444d18fdd5d58c8184358`.
- Atomic implementation commit `e540204fb` and evidence commit `b8ac12279` were pushed to `origin/audit-bt20-luna-20260910`; local and remote SHA both resolved to `b8ac12279665922d3184f94c024983979bc5b308` before delivery gates were awarded.
- Delivery gates were awarded collection-wide after the green collection, mechanism, typecheck, lint, format, manifest, and static checks plus the verified push. The ledger is 1020/1020 with 102/102 cards at 10/10.
- The final closeout evidence commit and Orca completion are the only remaining administrative steps at this checkpoint.

### Source reconciliation

Source: `docs/audits/BT20-reaudit/SOURCE-RECONCILIATION.md` (last commit 6e76b258c).

Reconciled on 2026-09-10 against the committed card catalog, the local rules/errata knowledge
base (`node tools/kb/query.mjs card <ID> --json`), the executable TypeScript modules, synchronized
IR manifest, focused behavioral tests, and peer engine tests.

- Scope: BT20-001 through BT20-102 (102 executable card records).
- Registration: 102/102 use `registerIrCard`; no BT20 module uses `registerCard`.
- Suppressions: no `@ts-nocheck` or `@ts-ignore` remains in the collection.
- Catalog correction: BT20-098 now contains the authoritative 2025-03-07 erratum, requiring
  exactly 9 total levels rather than “up to 9”; Q4439–Q4441 are covered behaviorally.
- Timing correction: BT20-097 uses the pre-leave `wouldLeavePlay` replacement window so its
  Dorumon source cost is paid from the intact triggering stack.
- Timing correction: BT20-102 drains the active attack window before another simultaneous
  End-of-Your-Turn attack may be declared, matching Q4419.
- Manifest reconciliation: `effects:sync:set` synchronized 102 BT20 records; the base-SHA check
  reports 37 semantic BT20 changes and zero semantic or byte changes outside BT20.
- Each card's exact source mapping, Q&A result, negative boundaries, and focused command are in
  `docs/audits/BT20-reaudit/BT20-<NNN>.md`.

No unresolved card-fidelity limitation remains.

### BT20-002 — Bebydomon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:31728-31743` identifies BT20-002 as a blue level-2 Digi-Egg. Its inherited clause is:

- `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` If this Digimon has `[Dracomon]`/`[Examon]` in its text, `Draw 1`.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-002` returns Q4281 (2025-06-13), which defines “in its text” as the complete printed union: name, traits, effects, inherited effects, Rule text, evolution requirements, DNA/DigiXros/burst/App Fusion/Link/Assembly requirements, and the listed icons. Q4282 (2025-01-24) confirms that a card with `[Dracomon]` in its text or `[Examon]` in its text satisfies this inherited effect. No erratum is returned.

The implementation correctly uses `match: "text"`, not exact-name or substring-only name matching. The natural stack now includes `BT21-046 Dracomon (X Antibody)`, whose name contains Dracomon but is not exactly Dracomon, demonstrating the complete-text rule; `BT20-025 Wingdramon` supplies the independent Examon-text arm. `BT11-023 Veemon` is the unrelated negative host.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-002.ts` contains no `@ts-nocheck`, no legacy `registerCard`, and registers exclusively through `registerIrCard("BT20-002", compiled)`. It reports `coverage: "full"` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| When Attacking timing | Inherited `trigger: "WhenAttacking"` | Natural public attacks after moving a legal evolved stack from breeding to battle |
| Once Per Turn | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` on the inherited effect | Natural first attack draws; same-turn second attack after a public unsuspend does not draw; next own turn draws again |
| Complete “in its text” predicate | `selfTopHasText` with Dracomon/Examon tokens and `match: "text"` | Dracomon-name variant, exact Dracomon-text and Examon-text hosts draw; unrelated Veemon host does not |
| Draw 1 | `Draw`, controller `mine`, amount 1 | Natural hand-size deltas and deck/security attack resolution |
| Legal evolution-stack source | Inherited effect resolves from the live top card while Bebydomon remains under the host | Natural stack reaches BT21-046 → Coredramon → Wingdramon and separately tests BT20-007 and Veemon hosts |

#### Defect corrected

No implementation defect was found in the inherited IR: the `match: "text"` predicate and once-per-turn identity are faithful to Q4281/Q4282. The test was strengthened to evolve Bebydomon publicly into `BT21-046 Dracomon (X Antibody)`, proving that a name containing Dracomon is valid under the complete-text rule, while retaining an independent Examon-text path and the unrelated Veemon negative.

All illegal Digi-Egg fixtures were removed from main decks and security stacks. The test now uses legal Digimon fillers (`BT1-010`/`BT1-015`) everywhere outside the legitimate Bebydomon breeding/evolution stacks. The public scenarios cover legal stacks, natural attack timing, same-turn refusal, next-own-turn reset, and negative near-match/non-text behavior.

#### Verification status

- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-002` — returned Q4281 and Q4282 as recorded above.
- The first focused run exposed a fixture interaction: the 1000-DP Dracomon host was deleted by a 4000-DP Greymon security card after the first attack, before the same-turn once-per-turn assertion could use it. The matching attack scenarios now use legal inert `BT1-090` Option security cards, preserving the natural security checks without an unrelated security battle.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT20/BT20-002.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — **1 file, 3/3 tests passed** in 581ms.
- Typecheck was not run in this lane.
- No engine, shared, catalog, or other card files were changed. No broad suite, typecheck, or delivery gate is claimed in this lane.

Provisional **8/10** (delivery gates fixed at 0): catalog/rules 2/2; IR trace 2/2; behavioral proof 2/2; peer/stack proof 2/2; delivery gates 0/2. Complete-text proof, once-per-turn/reset behavior, legal stacks, and fixtures are covered; the focused suite is green.

#### Baseline diagnosis and correction

The baseline focused suite had 5/6 tests passing and failed in the same-turn once-per-turn scenario while waiting for an `orderTriggers` decision containing BT20-003. The fixture put BT14-087 (Eiji) and BT20-003 in the same host stack. Because the printed Bibimon effect requires a host with no Tamer cards, the engine correctly omitted Bibimon at trigger collection while Eiji was still stacked; therefore the expected ordering prompt could not exist.

The test now uses a separate Eiji host and Bibimon host. Eiji can resolve first and then be selected by Bibimon into its own host, preserving the intended cross-effect sequence while keeping the no-Tamer condition true at declaration. This is a fixture correction; no engine or shared-data change was needed.

### BT20-007 — Dracomon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:31818-31840` identifies BT20-007 as a red level-3 Dragon Digimon (play cost 3, 1000 DP), with a normal red level-2 evolution cost of 0 and the following printed clauses:

- `[Digivolve] [Bebydomon]: Cost 0`.
- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` By trashing 1 card with `[Dracomon]`/`[Examon]` in its text in hand, draw 1 and gain 1 memory.
- Inherited `[Your Turn]` This Digimon gets +2000 DP.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-007` returns Q4290 (2025-01-24), which confirms that either a card with `[Dracomon]` in its text or one with `[Examon]` in its text satisfies the Start of Main Phase payment. Q4291 (2025-06-13) defines “in its text” as the union of name, traits, effects, inherited effects, Rule text, digivolution requirements, and the other listed combination requirements. No card-specific erratum or restriction is returned.

The bracketed evolution source is a specific card name, not a “name containing” condition. The IR requirement therefore uses `namesExact: ["Bebydomon"]`; the broader `names` field is reserved for printed “with [X] in its name” conditions. No catalog card provides a useful near-name Bebydomon variant for an isolated natural substring-vs-exact negative test, so the public positive evolution and structural exact-field assertion are the available proof.

The prior alternate evolution requirement encoded the printed `[Bebydomon]` source as `names`, which is substring matching in the card-definition resolver. It was corrected to `namesExact`, preserving the exact card-name semantics of the printed requirement. The structural test now asserts the exact field. The payment test's accepted branch previously fired `OnStartMainPhase` directly through the test helper; it now starts a real turn, waits for the main phase, and resolves the same public lifecycle as the decline and alternative tests.

The matching-text implementation remains `match: "text"`, as required by Q4291. The test exercises a Dracomon-text card, an Examon-text card, and a card containing neither, with the latter remaining in hand and memory unchanged.

#### Defect and fidelity findings

The removed ExVeemon is asserted in its owner’s trash, rather than incorrectly treating it as a DNA material. ### BT20-013 — BaoHuckmon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:31988-32008` identifies BT20-013 as a red level-4 Data Dinosaur Digimon with play cost 5, 5000 DP, and a red level-3 evolution route costing 2. Its printed clauses are:

- Inherited `[Your Turn]` All of your Digimon get +1000 DP.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-013` returns Q4293, Q4294, Q4295, and Q4296:

- Q4293 (2026-03-13) says two copies cannot combine simultaneous card-playing activations into one play or a total reduction of 4.
- Q4294 (2025-01-24) confirms that BaoHuckmon's reduction stacks with BT20-057 Gankoomon's own reduction, for a total reduction of 6.
- Q4295 (2025-01-24) says the effect can activate and play through ST12-03 Solarmon, but the play-cost reduction does not apply.
- Q4296 (2025-01-24) says the effect can activate through BT9-047 Pomumon's effect-play lock, but cannot use it to play the Digimon.

The printed phrase “with [Sistermon]/[Gankoomon] in its name” is a substring name reference, not an exact-name reference. The catalog includes variants such as Sistermon Ciel (Awakened) and Gankoomon (X Antibody), so `match: "name"` is correct. The target is separately constrained to `kind: ["Digimon"]`; this prevents the Chronicle Option BT23-099 The Sistermon Sisters Training Gym from being selected merely because its name contains Sistermon.

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-013.ts` has no `@ts-nocheck` or legacy registration and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard("BT20-013", compiled)`. It declares `coverage: "full"` and an empty residual list.

- `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` When your opponent's security stack is removed from, delete 1 of their Digimon with as much or less DP as this Digimon.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-020` returns Q4309, Q4665, Q4666, Q4667, Q4668, and Q6245:

- Q4309 establishes the priority of Security effects and security-removal watchers during a security check.
- Q4665-Q4668 define the player-scoped effect-play restriction, including that opponent Digimon/Tamers cannot be played by effects while the restriction is active, while an effect controlled by the restricting player may still play an opponent's card.
- Q6245 confirms the effect-play restriction also covers an opponent effect attempting to play a Digimon or Tamer into breeding.

Q4763 is not a BT20-020 ruling. The local KB assigns Q4763 to EX9-020 CresGarurumon, concerning that card's DNA replacement and whether the resulting Digimon leaves the battle area. It is not applicable to Imperialdramon: Fighter Mode, which has no Blast DNA, Alliance, or Blocker text. Those requested keywords likewise do not appear in BT20-020's catalog record and are not introduced here.

The references to `[Imperialdramon: Dragon Mode]` are bracketed card-name references, so they require exact printed-name matching. The catalog contains many cards with the same exact name across sets and also distinct Fighter/Paladin names; substring matching would incorrectly admit a hypothetical `Imperialdramon: Dragon Mode (X Antibody)` variant. The corrected module uses `nameExact` in both the alternate evolution requirement and the digivolution-stack condition.

### BT20-021 — Jesmon GX

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:32206-32244` identifies BT20-021 as a red/black level-7 Data Holy Warrior/X Antibody/Royal Knight ACE Digimon (play cost 9, 16000 DP), with red and black level-6 evolution routes costing 6 and Overflow -5. Its printed clauses are:

- Hand Counter Blast Digivolve.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` By placing 1 Royal Knight-trait card from hand or trash as this Digimon's bottom digivolution card, delete 1 opponent Digimon with DP as high as this Digimon.
- `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` Unsuspend this Digimon. Then, for every 2 Royal Knight-trait cards in this Digimon's digivolution cards, trash the opponent's top security card.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-021` returns Q4310 and Q4311. Q4310 confirms Blast Digivolve cannot use this card's DNA requirements (it has none); Q4311 confirms the two simultaneous When Attacking effects may be ordered by their controller.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-021.ts:7-144` contains no `@ts-nocheck` or legacy `registerCard`; it registers exclusively through `registerIrCard("BT20-021", compiled)`. Coverage is `full` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Counter Blast Digivolve | Hand-origin `Counter` effect with `BlastDigivolve` keyword | Structural assertion; public counter-window test accepts the card from hand and resolves its placement cost |
| Shared On Play/When Digivolving/When Attacking deletion | Three entry/attack effects each use `Delete` against an opponent Digimon with `dp lte relativeToSource`, paid by placing one own Royal Knight card from hand/trash at stack bottom; common `frequency: OncePerTurn` and `sharedUseKey` | Public play, evolution, and attack flows pay from both hand/trash, place at bottom, delete the inclusive DP boundary, preserve a 1-DP-over-boundary target, and prove the shared entry/attack reset across turns |
| Optionality and cost | Placement/deletion action is `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true`; payment is a real stack placement, not a memory shortcut | Public refusal leaves the Royal Knight in hand and the opponent target intact |
| When Attacking unsuspend + scaled security trash | Separate `WhenAttacking` effect is once per turn; unsuspends self, then trashes opponent top security with `per 2` scaling over Royal Knight digivolution cards | Natural public attacks resolve one and two security removals for three/four Royal Knight sources; simultaneous ordering is publicly exercised |
| Q4311 ordering | The two When Attacking effects are separate once-per-turn entries, allowing trigger ordering | Public attack ordering proof observes placement-before/unsuspend-before outcomes |
| ACE identity and routes | Catalog metadata exposes ACE, Overflow -5, red/black level-6 cost-6 routes | Metadata assertion and public Jesmon X → Jesmon GX stack |

#### Defect and fidelity findings

The prior colocated tests used direct `advance(...).fire(...)` calls at the security-trigger and On Play sites. Those structural probes have been replaced with public `attack` and `playCard` intents. The tests now wait for settled observable state, so the unsuspend/security-scaling and optional placement/deletion claims no longer depend on injected timing. Security assertions account for both mechanisms: the four-source attack removes the two effect-trash cards plus one ordinary attack check (4 → 1), while the one-source scaling case removes one effect-trash card plus one ordinary check and preserves the remaining security card in order (3 → 1).

The production IR faithfully keeps the placement/deletion action shared across all three printed timings while leaving the separate attack unsuspend/security action as its own once-per-turn effect. The relative-to-source DP ceiling is inclusive, the placement source is restricted to own Royal Knight-trait cards in hand/trash, and the placement is bottom-of-stack. DNA is not introduced into this card’s Blast Digivolve path, consistent with Q4310. The security scaler counts only digivolution cards, not the top card, and the public tests use real Royal Knight stacks including an Option source.

All deck/security fixtures use legal non-Digi-Egg cards, no `advance.fire`/`fireTiming` remains, and all effect assertions settle before inspection.

### BT20-033 — LoaderLeomon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:32551-32578` identifies BT20-033 as a yellow/black level-5 Vaccine Machine/ACCEL Ultimate Digimon with play cost 6, 6000 DP, and ordinary yellow or black level-4 evolution cost 4. Its printed clauses are:

- Alternate `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[ACCEL] trait: Cost 3`.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` until the end of your opponent's turn, 1 of their Digimon can't activate `[When Digivolving]` effects and gets -3000 DP.
- Inherited `[Opponent's Turn] [Once Per Turn]` when one of your opponent's Digimon attacks, you may change the attack target to this Digimon.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-033` returns five current Q&A entries:

- Q4326: “When Digivolving effects don't activate” prevents both triggering and effect-driven activation.
- Q4327: the restriction does not prevent the affected card's `[When Attacking]` half from activating.
- Q4328: another effect also cannot activate the affected card's `[When Digivolving]` effect.
- Q4329: the “by” condition of a restricted `[When Digivolving]` effect cannot be processed independently.
- Q4330: a combined `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` effect does not consume its once-per-turn use when blocked at `[When Digivolving]` timing.

There is no Security effect, named-card condition, trait target condition, or optional entry effect. The only optional clause is the inherited attack redirection, which is modeled as an optional action. The DP and restriction duration is the opponent's turn end, not a permanent or own-turn duration.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-033.ts` contains no `@ts-nocheck` or legacy `registerCard` and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard("BT20-033", compiled)`. Coverage is `full` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate ACCEL evolution | `digivolutionRequirement` level 4, trait ACCEL, cost 3, alternate | Public ACCEL stack succeeds with cost 3; unrelated level-4 source refuses |
| On Play restriction | `OnPlay` Restrict opponent Digimon count 1, `cannotActivateWhenDigivolving`, `untilOpponentTurnEnd` | Public play applies the restriction and prevents a target's When Digivolving effect |
| On Play DP reduction | `OnPlay` ModifyDP count 1, amount -3000, `untilOpponentTurnEnd` | Same public play lowers exactly one chosen opponent and expiry test restores DP after opponent turn end |
| When Digivolving clauses | Identical Restrict + ModifyDP actions under `WhenDigivolving` with `sameTarget: true` | Public evolution resolves both actions and the target-bound restriction/DP; later evolution after expiry resolves normally |
| Q4327/Q4330 combined timing boundary | Natural public evolution/attack of restricted BT20-021 Jesmon GX | Its combined When Digivolving/When Attacking effect is blocked on evolution (the required Royal Knight remains in hand), then places that card and deletes the victim on attack, proving the restriction does not block the attack branch or consume its once-per-turn use |
| Inherited redirection | Inherited `OpponentsTurn` → `whenOpponentAttacks` SubTrigger, optional self `RedirectAttack`, once per turn | Public opponent attacks redirect to host on acceptance, check security on refusal, redirect only once that turn, and reset on a later opponent turn |

#### Defects and corrections

No production IR defect was found. The existing module correctly uses the canonical restriction `cannotActivateWhenDigivolving`, target binding via `sameTarget`, `untilOpponentTurnEnd`, and an inherited optional attack redirect with source self targeting. The colocated structural proof was strengthened with catalog metadata and the exact ACCEL trait boundary is covered by legal and unrelated evolution sources.

The current Q&A boundary is proved through public behavior: LoaderLeomon suppresses a restricted target's public `[When Digivolving]` effect (Q4326/Q4328/Q4329), while a restricted BT20-021 Jesmon GX first has its combined `[When Digivolving]` branch blocked and then resolves its `[When Attacking]` branch on attack (Q4327/Q4330). The inherited redirect tests use natural public opponent attacks, including optional acceptance/refusal, once-per-turn same-turn refusal, and later-opponent-turn reset. No injected timing calls are present.

Fixtures use legal Digimon cards and ordinary security cards. No Digi-Egg is placed in a deck or security stack. Evolution tests assert successful costs and resulting stack identity; the ACCEL route uses a level-4 ACCEL Digimon and the negative route uses an unrelated level-4 source.

- `[All Turns]` when Tamer cards are placed in this Digimon's digivolution cards, activate 1 of this Digimon's `[When Digivolving]` effects. Then, 1 of your Digimon may attack an opponent's Digimon.
- Inherited `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` when your security stack is removed, if this Digimon has `[Fenriloogamon]` in its name, `＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞`.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-035` returns three current Q&A entries:

### BT20-044 — Breakdramon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT20-044 as a green/red level-6 Virus Machine Dragon Mega Digimon with play cost 12, 12000 DP, and ordinary green or red level-5 evolution cost 4. Its printed clauses are:

- Alternate `[Digivolve] [Groundramon]/[Wingdramon]: Cost 3`.
- `＜Blocker＞`.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Suspend 2 of your opponent's Digimon or Tamers. Then, 1 of your Digimon may attack.`
- `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` when any of your Digimon with `[Dracomon]/[Examon]` in their texts deletes an opponent's Digimon in battle, delete 1 of their suspended Digimon or Tamers.
- Inherited `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` with the same qualifying deletion, delete 1 of their suspended Digimon or Tamers.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-044` returns five current Q&A entries:

- Q4363: the resident effect is triggered by any of your Digimon with `[Dracomon]` or `[Examon]` in their texts.
- Q4364: the resident effect cannot activate when an opponent's Digimon and Breakdramon are deleted at the same timing.
- Q4365: the inherited effect has the same `[Dracomon]`/`[Examon]` text-bearing source boundary.
- Q4366: “X in its text” spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, Rule text, and evolution/DNA/DigiXros/burst/App Fusion/Link/Assembly requirements; `[Knightmon]` includes a card named `[DarkKnightmon]`.
- Q4367: the inherited effect cannot activate when the opponent's Digimon and the Digimon carrying Breakdramon leave at the same timing.

The bracketed Groundramon/Wingdramon evolution references are specific card names and therefore use exact-name semantics. The two entry effects target the opponent's Digimon/Tamer union and independently select two cards for suspension, while the optional attack targets one of the controller's Digimon. The deletion watchers target one opponent suspended Digimon/Tamer, last only for their current board state, and are once per turn with production turn reset.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-044.ts` contains no `@ts-nocheck` or legacy `registerCard` and registers exclusively with `registerIrCard("BT20-044", compiled)`. Coverage is `full` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Groundramon/Wingdramon alternate evolution | `digivolutionRequirement.namesExact`, both names, cost 3, alternate | Public Groundramon-stack evolution succeeds and retains the stack; exact matcher negatives reject name extensions |
| Blocker | Static keyword `Blocker` | Public played Breakdramon exposes Blocker |
| On Play / When Digivolving | Two trigger records, each `Suspend` opponent Digimon/Tamer count 2 then optional own-Digimon `Attack` | Structural trigger proof; public play suspends exactly two of three opponents and public play accepts the optional attack |
| Resident All Turns deletion watcher | OncePerTurn `whenDeletesInBattle`, own Digimon textContains `[Dracomon]`/`[Examon]`, source-not-deleted condition, delete one suspended opponent Digimon/Tamer | Public qualifying ally battle deletes a second suspended opponent; non-qualifying/opponent-controlled/simultaneous-deletion cases do not |
| Inherited All Turns deletion watcher | Same watcher contract with `isInherited: true`, OncePerTurn | Public inherited source deletes a second suspended opponent; repeat/reset coverage exercises both watcher locations |

#### Defects and corrections

The generated alternate evolution requirement used substring `names` for the bracketed `[Groundramon]/[Wingdramon]` references. It is corrected to `namesExact: ["Groundramon", "Wingdramon"]`, and colocated tests reject `Groundramon X`, `Groundramonmon`, `Wingdramon X`, and `Wingdramonmon`.

The resident and inherited watcher source filters correctly use `textContains` rather than exact source names: Q4363/Q4365/Q4366 define the full “in their texts” union. Both watchers also correctly require a non-deleted trigger source, matching Q4364/Q4367. The public suite covers the independent Digimon/Tamer target union, optional entry attack acceptance/refusal, once-per-turn same-turn suppression and next-turn reset for both resident and inherited watchers, the opponent-control and non-qualifying-source boundaries, and legal evolution stacks.

All deck and security fixtures use legal main-deck cards; no Digi-Egg is placed in a deck or security stack. No injected timing or direct effect firing is present.

### BT20-055 — Invisimon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies BT20-055 as a black/blue level-6 Virus Cyborg/LIBERATOR Mega Digimon with play cost 11, 11000 DP, and ordinary black or blue level-5 evolution cost 3. Its printed clauses are:

- `[Security] [End of Opponent's Turn]` play this card without paying the cost.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving] ＜De-Digivolve 2＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon and flip your opponent's top face-down security card face up. Then, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 1 or fewer digivolution cards.`
- `[Your Turn]` when your Digimon checks a face-up security card, you may place the top card of this Digimon face-up at the bottom of your security stack.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-055` returns Q4382–Q4388 and Q4722–Q4723:

- Q4382: when only the top opponent security card is face up, the second card is flipped.
- Q4383: effect-placed face-up security remains revealed and otherwise follows security rules.
- Q4384: a face-up security card is checked while remaining revealed.
- Q4385: a Security effect on a face-up checked card still triggers.
- Q4386: shuffling a stack turns face-up cards face down before shuffling.
- Q4387: Security effects take precedence on a simultaneous security check; other triggered effects follow turn-player activation order.
- Q4388: after Invisimon checks a face-up security card and its Your Turn effect moves its top stack card, the promoted card's End of Attack effect still triggers.
- Q4722: if the Security effect plays Invisimon during an opponent's end-of-turn attack and the player's security reaches zero, the player loses if the attack succeeds.
- Q4723: moving Invisimon's top card when its stack contains only Marvin Jackson turns the Digimon into a Tamer and removes the attacking Digimon.

The source self-reference in the Your Turn effect means the top card of this Invisimon is detached face up to the bottom of its controller's security stack. The De-Digivolve, security flip, and deletion are ordered as printed. There is no name/trait exact matcher, optionality on the Security effect, or once-per-turn/duration clause; only the Your Turn security movement is optional.

### BT20-057 — Gankoomon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:33260-33291` identifies BT20-057 as a black level-6 Data Holy Warrior/Royal Knight Digimon (play cost 12, 12000 DP), with black or red level-5 evolution cost 4. Its printed contract is:

- When this card would be played, if you have a Digimon with `[Huckmon]`/`[Jesmon]`/`[Sistermon]` in its name, reduce its play cost by 4.
- `Reboot` and `Blocker`.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` one of your Digimon may digivolve into a level-6-or-lower Digimon card with one of those names or the `Royal Knight` trait, from hand or trash, without paying the cost.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-057` returns Q4294 (2025-01-24). Its answer confirms that this card's self play-cost reduction stacks with the separate reduction from the effect that can play Gankoomon, for a total reduction of 6. The rendered question refers to a BT20-05 `[Gankoomon]` play effect, but the stacking principle is clear and does not change this card's isolated -4 clause. No other card-specific ruling or erratum is returned.

The printed “in its name” clauses correctly use name-substring matching (`match: "name"`), not exact-name matching. The `Royal Knight` clause is a trait match (`match: "trait"`). The free evolution destination is constrained to Digimon and level 6 or lower, and its source is hand or trash.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-057.ts` contains no `@ts-nocheck`, no legacy `registerCard`, and registers exclusively through `registerIrCard("BT20-057", compiled)`. It reports `coverage: "full"` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Self play reduction with allied named Digimon | Static self `wouldBePlayed` replacement, nested `reduceCost` amount 4, `youHave` friendly `kind: ["Digimon"]` and Huckmon/Jesmon/Sistermon `match: "name"` | Structural filter assertion; natural qualifying and non-qualifying play-cost scenarios |
| Reboot and Blocker | Two static keyword entries | Structural keyword assertion; natural public Blocker window and keyword observation |
| On Play free evolution | Optional `Digivolve`, target one friendly Digimon, destination Digimon level ≤6 with named or Royal Knight filter, `from: ["hand", "trash"]`, `payCost: false` | Natural play evolves a red level-5 into hand Jesmon; refusal leaves the target unchanged |
| When Digivolving free evolution | Same optional free-evolution IR under `WhenDigivolving` | Natural digivolution evolves a second level-5 into trash Alphamon (Royal Knight) |

#### Defect corrected

The public Blocker/Reboot fixture put `BT1-001` Yokomon (a Digi-Egg) in security. Digi-Eggs are not legal security cards, so the fixture now uses `BT1-090` Gravity Crush, a regular Option card, while preserving the same public block-window assertion. No implementation defect was found in the exact/name or trait predicates: the printed “in its name” wording is faithfully represented by `match: "name"`, while Royal Knight is represented as a trait match.

The focused tests already provide both free-evolution source zones (hand and trash), both entry timings, the named and Royal Knight destination arms, optional refusal, and a realistic stack/keyword combat path. No catalog card was found that would isolate a name-substring false positive without introducing another independent legality boundary; the structural matcher assertion and positive named paths cover the intended name semantics.

The catalog identifies a black level 6 Mega/Virus/Machine Digimon, play cost 12,
DP 12000, with a black level 5 evolution cost 4. The printed text is:

1. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with a play cost of 7 or less.`
2. `[All Turns] When this Digimon would leave the battle area, you may play 1 Digimon card with a play cost 11 or lower and the [Cyborg]/[Machine] trait from this Digimon's digivolution cards without paying the cost.`
3. `[DigiXros -2] [Raijinmon] x [Fujinmon] x [Suijinmon]`.

Q4391 asks what the All Turns effect can play. Its answer is one Digimon with
play cost 11 or lower and either the Cyborg trait or the Machine trait.

#### Implementation trace

| Printed behavior | IR | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| On Play deletion | `OnPlay` → `Delete`, opponent Digimon, `playCostLte: 7`, count 1 | Entry test exercises cost 7 deleted and cost 8 retained. |
| When Digivolving deletion | `WhenDigivolving` with the same filter | The same entry test runs both public play and public digivolve routes. |
| All Turns departure replacement | `AllTurns` → `Replacement`, `wouldLeavePlay`, self and battle-area source filter | Public Gaia Force departure tests resolve accepted and declined outcomes. |
| Optional free source play | Nested optional `PlayWithoutCost`, `fromOwnDigivolutionStack: true`, `payCost: false` | Q4391 acceptance/refusal tests use natural opponent Option play and settle the full effect stack. |
| Digimon, cost ≤11, Cyborg/Machine | Own-controller Digimon filter, `playCostLte: 11`, exact `match: "trait"` union | Positive Cyborg and Machine sources, a cost-12 Machine source, and a nonmatching card are covered. |
| DigiXros -2 | Three exact named material slots, `count: 2` | All-three public play proves six memory reduction and stack identity; each pair of distinct slots is also exercised, with a wrong-name rejection. |

The source module registers only `registerIrCard("BT20-058", compiled)`, has full
coverage and no residual IR. The existing IR is faithful; no module edit was
required.

#### Exact matching and stack evidence

The trait filter is an exact trait matcher, not a substring or kind match. The
positive source cases use BT9-042 Raijinmon (Cyborg) and BT9-029 Suijinmon
(Machine). BT20-058 itself is Machine but has play cost 12, so it is a useful
upper-bound negative. BT20-017 Jesmon is a legal Digimon fixture that is neither
Cyborg nor Machine and is used as a nonmatching DigiXros name. The DigiXros
requirement has three distinct named slots; the shared matcher permits any two
distinct slots, reducing each by two, which is why all three two-card pairs are
tested. No near-name variant is present in the committed catalog.

The stack cases use ordinary main-deck Digimon and prove source identity after
natural public DigiXros play and after a public Gaia Force departure. Accepted
replacement keeps only the selected eligible source in the battle area; refusal
and the cost-12 negative move the departing stack to trash without replay.
Fixtures contain no Digi-Egg in a deck or Security zone.

### BT20-060 — Alphamon: Ouryuken

#### Sources and printed contract

- Catalog: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` (`BT20-060`).
- Local rulings: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-060`.
- Implementation: `apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-060.ts`.
- Behavioral proof: `apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-060.test.ts`.

The catalog identifies a level 7, 16000 DP Alphamon: Ouryuken ACE with play cost
9, black/yellow/red colors, Mega/Vaccine form and attribute, and NO DATA/X
Antibody/Royal Knight/Chronicle traits. It has three level 6 evolution routes,
each costing 6 memory, is an ACE with Overflow -5, and prints:

1. `[Hand] [Counter] <Blast DNA Digivolve ([Alphamon] + [Ouryumon])>`.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] 1 of your opponent's Digimon gets -15000 DP until the end of their turn. Then, if DNA digivolving, trash your opponent's top security card and <Recovery +1 (Deck)>`.
3. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When security stacks are removed from, gain 3 memory.`

The KB returns Q4398, Q4399, and Q4726. Q4398 says a Digimon reaching 0 DP is
not deleted until the entire effect finishes. Q4399 gives the general ordering
of Security effects versus other simultaneous triggers. Q4726 confirms that a
turn-duration Rush grant from another card survives BT20-060's +3 memory event
when the turn does not immediately end.

#### Clause-to-IR-to-test trace

| Clause | IR | Behavioral proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Blast DNA from hand | `Counter`, `isFromHand: true`, `BlastDNADigivolve` keyword | Structural keyword test; public counter-window test offers and resolves the card only with Alphamon + Ouryumon material, and a missing-material test proves it is not offered. |
| -15000 to one opposing Digimon | Both `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` use one opposing Digimon target, `ModifyDP -15000`, `untilOpponentTurnEnd` | Public normal play and normal digivolve each reduce a 20000 DP target to 5000; the expiry test reaches the real end of the opponent’s turn and observes 20000 DP again. |
| DNA-only top-security trash | Each entry effect has `Trash` opponent security top, gated by `isDnaDigivolving` | Normal play/evolution retain the opponent’s two security cards; the public Blast DNA test observes the opponent’s top card trashed. |
| DNA-only Recovery +1 | Each entry effect grants self `Recovery 1`, gated by `isDnaDigivolving`, permanent keyword | The public Blast DNA test observes the recovered card on top of the owner’s security and confirms the recovery event precedes the delayed 0-DP deletion. |
| All Turns once-per-turn +3 memory | `AllTurns` → `SubTrigger whenSecurityRemoved` → `GainMemory 3`, `frequency: OncePerTurn` | A natural ST1-07 Security Attack +1 checks two cards from the owner’s security in one attack; memory increases only once. |
| ACE Overflow -5 | Catalog metadata, exercised by engine’s normal removal path | Public battle against a 20000 DP attacker deletes the ACE and observes -5 memory and the card in trash. |

The module registers only `registerIrCard("BT20-060", compiled)`, has
`coverage: "full"`, and an empty residual list. No exact name/trait matcher is
used by this card, so there is no matcher approximation or near-name boundary
to add. The implementation is faithful and required no production edit.

#### Public-flow and ruling evidence

The former direct `fireSubTrigger("whenSecurityRemoved", ...)` proof was
replaced with a public attack by a BT20-057 stack carrying ST1-07 underneath;
ST1-07's inherited Security Attack +1 naturally removes two inert BT1-090
security Options. This proves the once-per-turn deduplication at the
observable game-state boundary without a timing seam. Because the attacker is
seat 1 and the card is owned by seat 0, the owner's +3 is observed as -3 on
the global memory gauge.
The Overflow test likewise no longer calls a privileged delete verb: an
opponent’s public attack deletes the ACE in battle.

Q4398 is covered by the public Blast DNA counter scenario. It records event
ordering: security trash and Recovery complete before the 0-DP target is moved
to trash. Q4399 is a general simultaneous-trigger ordering ruling; BT20-060 has
no `[Security]` effect, so its ordering comparison is not a card-specific
clause and is documented rather than misrepresented as independently tested.
Q4726 concerns a Rush grant from another card’s End of Your Turn effect; this
card grants no Rush and has no End of Your Turn effect, so that cross-card
duration interaction is outside this lane’s executable contract.

All stacks and fixtures use ordinary main-deck Digimon/cards; no Digi-Egg is
placed in deck or Security. The public counter proof uses a legal Alphamon base
and Ouryumon material, while normal play/evolution tests exercise a legal
level-6 route and its exact memory cost; the catalog metadata assertion records
all three printed color routes.

### BT20-078 — Reapermon

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:33866-33895` identifies BT20-078 as a purple/black level-6 Virus Digimon with Cyborg and Ghost traits (play cost 11, 11000 DP), with purple and black level-5 evolution routes costing 3. Its printed clauses are:

The KB returns Q4401 and Q4402. Q4401 says Reapermon’s All Turns effect and the opponent’s When Digivolving effect trigger simultaneously, with the turn player activating first. Q4402 limits the watcher to effect-driven Digivolutions (for example P-103 Offense Training), excluding ordinary non-digivolution effects such as treating a Tamer as a Digimon.

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-078.ts:6-45` is compiled IR only and registers exactly once through `registerIrCard("BT20-078", compiled)`. There is no `@ts-` suppression or legacy `registerCard`; `coverage` is `full` and `residual` is empty.

| Q4401 activation order | SubTrigger action is registered as Reapermon’s observer of the effect-driven evolution | Public training flow checks the evolution effect event occurs before Reapermon’s trigger |
| On Deletion boundary | Mandatory Delete targets opponent Digimon/Tamer with `playCostLte: 4`, count 1 | Public combat deletion removes cost-3 Agumon and preserves cost-6 MetalGreymon and cost-5 Tamer cases |

#### Defect and fidelity findings

The prior test suite relied on direct turn-seat/count/memory mutations and `advance.fire(EffectTiming.OnDeletion)`. Those seams were removed. The effect-driven watcher now runs through a natural public turn loop and P-106 Delay activations; the ordinary evolution negative also runs in an opponent Main phase. The >4 play-cost boundary now deletes Reapermon through public combat and observes that high-cost targets survive. The public Blocker and Collision flows remain intact.

Catalog identity, evolution routes, effect text, complete coverage, and empty residual assertions were added. Fixtures use legal Digimon/Option cards; no Digi-Egg is placed in a main deck or security. No injected timing (`EffectTiming` or `advance.fire`) remains in the card test.

| Q4410/Q4411 source and cost | `PlayWithoutCost` uses `fromOwnDigivolutionStack: true`, exact Omekamon name, and suspending self as the cost | Acceptance suspends the breeding source and plays its own stack card; refusal leaves it in breeding; an already-suspended source cannot activate |

Bracketed card-name references were widened with substring `name` matching. The module now uses exact matching for `[Omnimon (X Antibody)]`, `[King Drasil_7D6]`, and `[Omekamon]`, with colocated exact-name positive/near-name negative proof. The inherited effect remains restricted to breeding and to the owner’s security-removal event, preserving Q4409–Q4411 boundaries.

The test suite adds complete catalog identity/effect/coverage assertions and replaces direct turn-seat mutation with natural public turn-loop phases for Blocker, deletion, security-removal, battle-area negative, and suspended-source negative flows. ### BT20-084 — Sistermon Ciel (Awakened)

#### Contract and rules evidence

- Catalog source: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`, card `BT20-084`.
- Catalog contract: White level 4 Digimon, play cost 5/6000 DP; alternate `[Digivolve] [Sistermon Ciel]: Cost 1`; `[Trash] [All Turns]` when any of your Digimon are played, one of your Sistermon Ciel Digimon may digivolve into this card from trash without paying the cost; `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` one opponent's Digimon or Tamer can't suspend until the end of that opponent's turn; `[End of All Turns]` place this Digimon's top stacked card as the top security card.
- Knowledge-base source: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-084`.
- Q&A `Q4412` (2025-01-24): a `[Trash]` effect can trigger/activate only while its card is in trash.
- Q&A `Q4413` (2025-01-24): this trash effect and the played Sistermon Ciel's On Play effect trigger simultaneously and may be ordered by the player.
- Q&A `Q4414` (2025-01-24): if the trash effect resolves first and evolves the played Sistermon Ciel, that played card is no longer a Sistermon Ciel and its pending On Play effect cannot activate.

#### Clause → IR → behavioral proof

| Printed clause | IR / implementation | Focused proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Trash] [All Turns] When any of your Digimon are played` | `effects[0]`: `isFromTrash: true`, `trigger: "AllTurns"`, `SubTrigger(event: "whenPlayed")`, `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"`, `kind: ["Digimon"]` | Playing an own Digimon evolves the trash card; playing an own Tamer does not; simultaneous played-Ciel ordering is tested both ways |
| One of your Sistermon Ciel may digivolve into this card from trash without paying | `Digivolve.target.controller: "mine"`, `nameExact` Sistermon Ciel; `into` BT20-084 with `controller: "mine"`, `zone: "trash"`; `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false`, `ignoreRequirements: true`, `optional: true` | Positive evolution, optional refusal, exact target exclusion for already Awakened Ciel, and Q4413/Q4414 ordering tests |
| `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` one opponent's Digimon or Tamer can't suspend | Both entry effects use `Restrict`, `restriction: "suspend"`, `count: 1`, `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"`, and `target.filter.controller: "opponent"`, `kind: ["Digimon", "Tamer"]` | Both play and alternate-evolution routes now include a same-controller ally and an opposing target; own ally remains suspendable while selected opposing Digimon/Tamer is blocked |
| `[End of All Turns]` place this Digimon's top stacked card as top security | `SecurityManipulation(op: "placeAsSecurity", controller: "mine", source: self, fromDigivolutionTop: true, toTop: true)` | Real `runOneTurn()` end-of-turn test moves the stacked card to security and leaves the host stack empty |
| Alternate `[Digivolve] [Sistermon Ciel]: Cost 1` | `digivolutionRequirement.namesExact: ["Sistermon Ciel"]`, `cost: 1`, `isAlternate: true` | A legal BT6-084 Sistermon Ciel evolves for one memory; near-name BT7-083 Sistermon Ciel (Awakened) is rejected without changing hand, stack, or memory |

The opponent controller filters were present in both production restriction effects and were retained unchanged; the expanded behavioral test now proves the filters against allied Digimon/Tamers as well as opposing Digimon/Tamers. The trash watcher and evolution target also retain the required own-controller filters.

The direct module is compiled IR with `coverage: "full"` and `residual: []`. It contains exactly one `registerIrCard("BT20-084", compiled)`, with no `registerCard` and no `@ts-nocheck`.

#### Corrections

1. The alternate evolution requirement used `names`, a substring match. Because the bracketed `[Sistermon Ciel]` requirement names an exact card, this incorrectly admitted `Sistermon Ciel (Awakened)` peers such as BT7-083. It now uses `namesExact: ["Sistermon Ciel"]`.
2. The End of All Turns fixture put Digi-Egg `BT1-001` in security, which is not a legal security card. It now uses main-deck Digimon `BT1-009`.
3. The restriction test now includes an allied permanent and checks that it remains suspendable, while checking an opposing Digimon on the play route and an opposing Tamer on the evolution route.

### BT20-085 — Shoto Kazama

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record identifies BT20-085 as the green Tamer Shoto Kazama (play cost 3,
LIBERATOR trait, maximum four copies). Its printed clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` By returning this Tamer to the bottom of the deck, you may play one `[Shoto Kazama]` from hand without paying its cost.
- Then, if you don't have a Digimon, you may play one level 3 Digimon card with `[Avian]/[Bird]` in any of its traits from trash without paying its cost.
- `[End of Your Turn]` By suspending this Tamer, suspend one of your opponent's Digimon and, until the end of their turn, one of your Digimon with the `[Vortex Warriors]` trait gets +2000 DP.
- `[Security]` Play this card without paying its cost.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-085` returns Q5553 and Q5554. Q5553 confirms
that the part after “Then” cannot be processed unless the Tamer was returned to
the bottom of the deck. Q5554 confirms that a Shoto played by this start-of-main
effect does not activate another start-of-main effect in the same timing window.
Q4409–Q4411 are indexed for BT20-083's breeding/security rulings, not BT20-085,
so they are not applicable to this card.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-085.ts` is compiled IR only and registers exactly
once through `registerIrCard("BT20-085", compiled)`. It has `coverage: "full"`,
an empty residual, and no legacy registration or TypeScript suppression.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start-of-main return and free exact-name Shoto play | `PlayWithoutCost` from hand, own exact `nameExact` Shoto target, optional, with a self return-to-`deckBottom` cost and `abortOnDecline` | Natural start-of-main loop proves refusal leaves the Tamer, replacement, and trash card in place; acceptance proves the original instance is bottom deck and the selected replacement instance is played |
| Q5553 gated Then clause | Second `PlayWithoutCost` from trash, level 3, own Digimon, Avian/Bird trait, with `allOf(ifThisEffectActed, youHaveNone)` | The refusal case leaves the Avian/Bird card in trash; the accepted case plays it after the Tamer leaves |
| Q5554 no same-window re-entry | Start timing is resolved through the public turn lifecycle; the accepted case includes a second Shoto in hand and proves it remains there | Accepted natural-flow test proves only the selected replacement enters play |
| End-of-turn opposing suspension | `Suspend` targets one opponent Digimon and requires suspending this Tamer as a cost | Public end-of-turn flow observes both the Tamer and the sole opposing Digimon suspended before the opponent's natural unsuspend phase |
| Vortex Warriors +2000 duration | Separate own Digimon trait target, `ModifyDP +2000`, `untilOpponentTurnEnd` | Public next opponent turn observes 9000 DP, then the following own main phase observes the base 7000 DP |
| Security play | Security `PlayWithoutCost` of self with `payCost: false` | Public attack/security check proves the exact security instance enters the opponent's battle area without changing memory |

#### Defects and fidelity corrections

The hand-authored IR used substring `match: "name"` for the bracketed
`[Shoto Kazama]` reference. It now uses `nameExact`; the test includes a direct
near-name negative (`Shoto Kazama Jr.`) and checks the catalog and printed text.
The injected `advance.fireGlobal(EffectTiming.StartOfYourMainPhase)` proofs were
replaced by `startTurnLoop()` and `waitForMainPhase(0)`. The end-of-turn test now
uses the public `runOneTurn()` result and the emitted `effectResolved` record
(`timing: "OnEndTurn"`) to
prove that the suspension effect completed, then continues from the observed
seat-1 Main phase through a natural turn loop. It no longer mutates `turnSeat` or
`memory`, and observes duration expiry. The opposing Digimon is not incorrectly
asserted to remain suspended after its natural unsuspend phase.
All deck/security fixtures use main-deck
Digimon and contain no Digi-Eggs.

### BT20-086 — Altea

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The committed catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:34059-34083` identifies BT20-086 as Altea: a black LIBERATOR Tamer, play cost 4, with no evolution cost or inherited text. Its printed clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Turn]` If memory is 2 or less, set it to 3.
- `[Start of Your Main Phase]` By placing one black Digimon card with the `[Cyborg]`/`[Machine]` trait and play cost 4 or less from hand or trash at the bottom of an own Digimon with that trait, flip the opponent's top face-down security card face up.
- `[Security]` Play this card without paying the cost.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-086` returns Q4422-Q4427. Q4422 confirms the source trait is a union (Cyborg or Machine) with the black and cost-4 ceiling. Q4423 selects the first face-down security card from the top when earlier cards are already face up. Q4424 says face-up security remains revealed, Q4425 says a check does not re-hide it, Q4426 says its Security effect still activates, and Q4427 says a security shuffle re-hides every face-up card.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-086.ts` is compiled IR only and registers exactly once with `registerIrCard("BT20-086", compiled)`. It declares `coverage: "full"` and `residual: []`; no legacy registration or TypeScript suppression is present.

| Printed clause / ruling | IR representation | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start-of-turn memory setter | `StartOfYourTurn` `SetMemory` value 3 with `memoryAtMost` 2 and own controller | Public `startTurnLoop` boundary test at memory 2, 3, and 4 |
| Start-of-main placement and flip | `StartOfYourMainPhase` `SecurityManipulation flipFaceUp` for opponent, with optional abortable `place` cost at stack bottom | Public hand and trash placement under a Machine host, with source card leaving its zone and security changing |
| Q4422 source/host union and boundaries | Source is own Digimon, black, play cost ≤4, Cyborg/Machine trait; host is own Digimon with Cyborg/Machine trait | Cyborg and Machine eligible cards both place; wrong-trait, non-black, and over-cost cards remain in hand |
| Q4423 first face-down selection | Shared `flipFaceUp` operation | Security `[face-up, face-down, face-down]` becomes `[face-up, face-up, face-down]` |
| Q4424-Q4426 face-up check | Public security check of a face-up ST1-12 | `securityChecked` has `resolution: "effect"`; ST1-12 plays and the card was never re-hidden |
| Q4427 shuffle reset | Shared security shuffle semantics (public BT15-092 effect) | Two face-up security cards become face-down after the public shuffle |
| Security play | Security effect is a self-targeted `PlayWithoutCost`, `payCost: false` | Structural Security branch assertion; runtime Security play is covered by the shared checked-card behavior |

#### Defect and fidelity findings

The prior test relied on `runOneTurn`, direct `turnSeat`/memory mutation, and a private `mainPhase` cast. Those are timing-only or direct-state seams. The audit replaces them with `startTurnLoop`, `advance(...).waitForMainPhase`, public intents, and surrender, while retaining only direct memory assignment as a fixture for the printed start-of-turn boundary. The source IR already had the correct black, cost, trait, hand/trash, host, bottom-stack, and face-up operation constraints; no production change was required.

The placement action is intentionally optional and abortable: the printed “By placing…” payment is a controller choice under the engine's cost-processing convention used by neighboring audited cards (for example BT19-081/BT19-086). The test proves both acceptance and refusal, including the all-or-nothing boundary that refusal leaves the candidate and security unchanged.

Fixtures use legal main-deck Digimon/Tamers/Options only; no Digi-Egg appears in deck or Security. No exact card-name matcher is printed on Altea, so no near-name proof is applicable.

### BT20-090 — Yuuki

#### Contract and source reconciliation

The catalog record at `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json:34131-34145` identifies BT20-090 as a purple LIBERATOR Tamer with play cost 4. Its printed clauses are:

- `[Start of Your Turn]` If you have 2 or less memory, set it to 3.
- `[End of Your Turn]` If you have 4 or fewer cards in hand, by suspending this Tamer, one of your Digimon with the `Dark Dragon` or `Evil Dragon` trait attacks a player.
- `[Security]` Play this card without paying the cost.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-090` returns Q4431 (2025-04-04). It rules that when two copies trigger simultaneously at end of turn, the second effect cannot declare a new attack during the first attack, even if its effect is activated before the counter timing. No additional erratum or card-specific ruling is returned.

There are no exact-name references on this card. The attacker population is correctly a trait union (`match: "trait"`) and is constrained to own, unsuspended Digimon; the Security clause is a self-reference and does not need name matching.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-090.ts` contains no `@ts-nocheck`, no legacy `registerCard`, and registers exclusively through `registerIrCard("BT20-090", compiled)`. It reports `coverage: "full"` with an empty residual list.

| Printed clause | IR | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start of Your Turn memory setting | `StartOfYourTurn` → `SetMemory(3)` gated by `memoryAtMost(2)` | Natural `runOneTurn()` lifecycle observes 1 → 3; natural negative observes 5 unchanged |
| End of Your Turn conditional attack | `EndOfYourTurn` → optional player attack, `handAtMost(4)`, cost suspending this Tamer, target one own unsuspended Digimon with Dark Dragon/Evil Dragon trait | Natural turn lifecycle suspends Yuuki and the selected DarkLizardmon; security decreases by one |
| Q4431 multiple-copy timing | Independent per-copy End-of-Turn effects and public attack flow | Natural two-copy scenario exercises simultaneous triggers and observes both Tamers/qualifying Digimon and the resulting security state |
| Security play | Security self-targeted `PlayWithoutCost`, source `security`, `payCost: false` | Natural public security check plays the exact BT20-090 instance into the battle area for free |
| Identity and metadata | Catalog-aligned purple LIBERATOR Tamer, play cost 4 | Structural catalog assertion |

#### Defect corrected

The memory tests previously monkey-patched the private `fireTiming` method solely to capture the post-`OnStartTurn` memory value. That injected timing seam has been removed. Both tests now start a real turn, wait for the public Main-phase readiness boundary, assert the memory result, and close the phase through the public `endPhase` intent. The helper used by the end-turn tests likewise now uses `advance(...).waitForMainPhase` and `endMainPhaseIfOpen` rather than private phase polling.

The End-of-Turn fixtures placed `BT1-001` Yokomon Digi-Eggs in security. Digi-Eggs are not legal security cards. All such fixtures now use `BT1-090` Gravity Crush, a legal inert Option, preserving the security count and attack flow.

The structural test now explicitly checks the memory gate, hand-size gate, own unsuspended Digimon trait union, attack-player target, self-suspension cost, and catalog identity. The natural tests cover both threshold boundaries, the Q4431 multiple-copy scenario, and exact-instance Security play.

#### Contract and rules evidence

The committed catalog identifies BT20-101 as the green level-6 Mega Zephagamon:

alternate evolution from a level-6 Vortex Warriors Digimon with play cost 10 or
higher for 1. It is an Ace with Overflow -4 and has Magic Knight, Vortex
Warriors, LIBERATOR, and Bird Dragon traits.

Its printed clauses are:

- `[Digivolve]` play cost 10 or higher Lv.6 with the `[Vortex Warriors]` trait:
  cost 1;
- `[Hand] [Counter]` `<Blast Digivolve>`;
- `<Piercing>`, `<Vortex>`, and `<Blocker>`;
- `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` when any Digimon suspends, this Digimon may
  unsuspend;
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` you may suspend 1 Digimon, then for every 2
  suspended Digimon you may return 1 of your opponent's suspended Digimon to the
  bottom of the deck.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-101` returns Q4415 and Q4416. Q4415 confirms
that either player's Digimon suspension triggers the All Turns effect. Q4416
confirms that the On Play/When Digivolving suspension may target either player's
Digimon.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-101.ts` is compiled IR with exactly one
`registerIrCard("BT20-101", compiled)` registration, `coverage: "full"`, and an
empty residual. The hand-fixed source filter intentionally uses
`controllerDefault: "any"` for the `whenSuspended` watcher and its scaling
filter; this is required by Q4415 and the unqualified “every 2 suspended
Digimon” wording. The suspend target likewise uses any controller, while the
return target is specifically an opponent's suspended Digimon and moves to
`deckBottom`. The watcher is `OncePerTurn`, its unsuspend is optional, and the
two entry triggers carry the same ordered suspend-then-scale return actions.
The alternate evolution requirement is represented separately with level 6,
Vortex Warriors, base play cost minimum 10, and cost 1.

#### Clause-to-proof map

| Printed clause | IR representation | Colocated proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate evolution | `digivolutionRequirement` level 6, `traits: ["Vortex Warriors"]`, `basePlayCostMin: 10`, cost 1 | Catalog/helper boundary plus public evolution from EX11-035 checks cost, resulting top card, and stack |
| Blast Digivolve and keywords | Counter keyword plus Static Piercing/Vortex/Blocker keyword entries | Public Counter response Blast Digivolves in an attack window; Blocker redirects the attack and Piercing later performs a security check |
| All Turns, any suspension, once per turn | `SubTrigger(whenSuspended)` with any-controller Digimon source filter, optional self Unsuspend, `frequency: OncePerTurn` | Public own attack, a second same-turn attack, and a natural opponent-turn attack prove acceptance, once-per-turn suppression, and next-turn reset; refusal leaves the attacker suspended |
| On Play target and scaling | Optional any-controller Digimon Suspend followed by optional opponent suspended-Digimon Return, scaling by every 2 suspended Digimon | Public On Play with an allied target and two opposing suspended Digimon checks exact target ownership and one bottom-deck return; 0/1/2 boundary matrix proves no return below two |
| When Digivolving reuse | Same ordered actions on the `WhenDigivolving` trigger | Public alternate evolution repeats the suspend/return behavior and checks its live stack and zones |
| Bottom-deck destination | `Return.to: "deckBottom"` | Runtime deck length and returned instance identity prove the opposing card left the battle area for the deck |

The tests use legal main-deck cards only and no Digi-Egg in deck or security.
They contain no injected timing calls or direct turn-seat mutation. Turn-sensitive
proofs use `startTurnLoop`, `waitForMainPhase`, public attack/evolution/counter
intents, and settled observable events/zones.

The unsuspend proof uses the harness's public auto-accept/auto-select responders,
then inspects `s.decisions` to prove that the actual On Play optional and target
requests included the watcher. Starting the watcher unsuspended ensures the
public On Play suspend creates the real transition; a subsequent same-turn
attack proves the once-per-turn effect is not reused.

### BT20-102 — Omnimon (X Antibody)

#### Contract and rules evidence

The committed catalog identifies BT20-102 as a level 7, 16-cost, 16000-DP blue/white/red Digimon. It is Mega/Vaccine and has Holy Warrior, X Antibody, Royal Knight, and LIBERATOR traits. Its ordinary evolution routes are blue or red level 6 for cost 6; its alternate route is `[Digivolve] [Omnimon]: Cost 2`. The printed effects are:

- `<Raid>`, `<Piercing>`, and `<Blocker>`.
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` if an `[Omnimon]` or `[X Antibody]` card is in this Digimon's digivolution cards, choose one Digimon belonging to both players and delete all other Digimon; then return one opposing Digimon to the bottom of its owner's deck.
- `[End of Your Turn] [Once Per Turn]` one own Digimon may gain `<Rush>` for the turn and attack without suspending.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT20-102` returns Q4417, Q4418, Q4419, Q4725, Q4726, Q5907, and Q6018:

- Q4417: after choosing the Rush Digimon, its attack cannot be declined when possible.
- Q4418: a suspended Digimon may be chosen for the unsuspending attack.
- Q4419: simultaneous copies cannot create a second attack while the first attack is in progress.
- Q4725: the `Then` return still resolves when the first conditional delete process did not apply.
- Q4726: Rush lasts through a security-triggered +3-memory event that prevents the turn from ending.
- Q5907: when another effect plays BT20-102 under its former source, the newly played card's stack satisfies its own `[Omnimon]/[X Antibody]` condition.
- Q6018: an activated entry effect continues through its `Then` process even if an immediate effect removes its source.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-102.ts` registers exclusively through `registerIrCard("BT20-102", compiled)`, has `coverage: "full"`, and has no residual list. The alternate evolution requirement is encoded as `namesExact: ["Omnimon"]`, cost 2. Both entry timings use the same conditional `Delete` plus unconditional `Return` sequence. The Delete uses a nested `except` selector to preserve the chosen survivor; the target is all Digimon on both sides, while Return is one opposing Digimon to `deckBottom`. End-of-turn uses an optional Rush grant, `sameTarget: true`, and a dependent unsuspending Attack under `OncePerTurn`.

| Printed clause | IR | Public proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Stats, colors, traits, evolution costs | catalog metadata and `digivolutionRequirement` | catalog assertion and alternate-route assertion |
| Raid/Piercing/Blocker | three Static keyword entries | structural keyword test |
| Conditional entry gate | `selfDigivolutionStackMatchesFilter` for exact `[Omnimon]` or `[X Antibody]` | stack matrix with trait-only, exact-name, and Rule Name cases |
| Choose one survivor from both players | Delete `count: "all"` with nested `except` count 1/selector any | public evolution preserves the selected host and deletes all other Digimon |
| Return one opposing Digimon after delete process | unconditional Return, controller opponent, `deckBottom` | public evolution places the selected opposing survivor at deck bottom |
| Q4725 `Then` when conditional delete is skipped | condition only gates Delete; Return remains separate | public play without qualifying stack keeps own board and bottoms exactly one opposing Digimon |
| End-of-turn optional Rush | optional `GainKeyword`, `forTheTurn`, `abortOnDecline` | public end-of-turn positive and optional-refusal tests |
| Attack same chosen Digimon without suspending | dependent `Attack`, `sameTarget`, `withoutSuspending: true` | positive unsuspended and Q4418 suspended Digimon tests |
| Once-per-turn and simultaneous-copy boundary | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`; attack drains the active timing window | Q4419 two-copy end-of-turn test slices from a baseline and counts exactly one new `attackDeclared` and one security check; BT20-102 has no Security Attack +1, so the one seeded security card is removed |

Exact bracket-name behavior is tested in a mixed stack: trait-only `BT9-008` does not satisfy the exact `[X Antibody]` name condition, while the exact-name `BT9-109` Option and EX5-070 Rule Name do. The alternate `[Omnimon]` evolution requirement is separately asserted as an exact name requirement. Fixtures use legal main-deck/security cards only; no Digi-Egg is placed in deck or security.

#### Q&A and seam accounting

Q4417, Q4418, Q4419, and Q4725 have direct behavioral coverage in the colocated test. Q5907 is a cross-card source-identity scenario owned by the card that plays BT20-102 under itself; this lane records it as a named cross-card seam, while the local stack-condition matcher is covered by the public evolution matrix. Q6018 depends on an immediate removal during an already-running multi-action effect and is recorded as an engine continuation seam; the local test proves the ordinary conditional/Then separation. Q4726 requires BT20-060's security-removal watcher and is recorded as a named peer interaction seam; this lane does not alter BT20-060 or claim an isolated BT20-102 test proves that external effect.

#### Test evidence

The coordinator recorded the exact focused command as green in `RUN.md`:

```bash
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT20/BT20-102.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
```

Static checks performed after the rewrite:

```bash
pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-102.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT20/BT20-102.test.ts docs/audits/BT20-reaudit/BT20-102.md
git diff --check
```

Result: the coordinator-focused suite is green; no typecheck was run by this lane.

#### Defects and rubric

- Removed all direct `advance.fireForPermanent(...)` timing injections from the prior On Play tests.
- Removed direct `turnSeat` mutation and replaced both End-of-Your-Turn proofs with `startTurnLoop()`, `waitForMainPhase()`, and public phase closure. Attack assertions now use saved `attackDeclared` and `securityRevealed` events keyed to the permanent ID, because the turn loop clears the transient `hasAttackedThisTurn` observation by the time final state is read. Q4419 slices attack events from a baseline captured immediately before End-of-Your-Turn, avoiding unrelated setup events. Q4418 captures the suspended state through the observable event hook at declaration, before the next Active phase can unsuspend it. The declined path ends by surrendering only after its assertions; the positive paths close the opponent phase publicly before final cleanup.
- The End-of-Your-Turn Attack now sets `drainTimingWindowDuringAttack: true`. This is the card-local IR seam required by Q4419: simultaneous copies are activated before the first attack's Counter timing, and the second copy's Attack action is rejected while the first attack is in flight. BT20-102 has no Security Attack +1; the regression therefore asserts one new declaration and one check, not two checks interpreted as one attack.

## Mechanisms

No engine seam document was produced for BT20. No standalone engine seam document was written. Shared timing, evolution, targeting and continuous-effect corrections made during the run are recorded per card under Card ledger and in Source reconciliation.
